# Provenance

Irie is written from public documentation and public open-source projects. Every source that shaped the code is listed here: what it is, when it was read, and what it was used for.

## Sources

| Date | Source | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-26 | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md | Hook events and payload fields (`agent_id`, `transcript_path`, `notification_type`), `additionalContext`, hook timeouts and `onFailure: "block"`: the hook shim, hold at the next tool call, context notes, state mapping (DESIGN §3.3, §4.3) |
| 2026-10-26 | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings.md | `--settings` as a per-launch layer whose hook lists merge with the user's: per-session hook injection without editing user files (DESIGN §3.3) |
| 2026-10-26 | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference.md | Launch flags (`--model`, `--effort`, `--permission-mode`, `--session-id`, `--resume`): default profile argv and reopen (DESIGN §3.3) |
| 2026-10-26 | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/interactive-mode.md | Enter queues for the next tool boundary, Ctrl+X Ctrl+S sends now, Ctrl+Z behaviour: notes go through hook context; freeze uses signals (DESIGN §3.2, §4.3) |
| 2026-10-26 | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/terminal-config.md | tmux options Claude Code needs (`allow-passthrough`, `focus-events`, `extended-keys`): generated tmux.conf (DESIGN §2.3) |
| 2026-10-26 | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-directory.md ; https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sessions.md | Transcript and subagent transcript locations: transcript tailer and subagent mirrors (DESIGN §4.4) |
| 2026-10-26 | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees.md | `.worktreeinclude` convention for copying untracked files into worktrees: worktree rules (DESIGN §4.2) |

## Third-party code

No third-party code is copied into the repository, but for the Mac app's Ghostty terminal, which adapts parts of Ghostty's own macOS app (MIT; its row below), and xterm.js, which Irie for Windows' page carries in `internal/winapp/web/vendor/xterm` (MIT; its row below). The demo recordings (docs/demo) run vhs (MIT) and the tools it drives, tmux, git and ffmpeg; none of them is copied or compiled in. These Go modules are compiled into `irie`, fetched by the Go toolchain at the versions pinned in `go.mod` and checked against `go.sum` (`go mod verify`). Bubble Tea, Bubbles, Lip Gloss and TOML are direct dependencies (DESIGN §8.1); the rest come in through them. M2 and M3 add no dependency: M3 runs the user's own `git` for repos and worktrees. M10 makes `golang.org/x/sys`, already compiled in, a direct dependency, and runs the user's own `ollama`; it bundles neither Ollama nor any model.

The skills proposal, [docs/proposals/skills.md](docs/proposals/skills.md), names third-party skills by repository, path, commit and file hash. None of their text is copied into the repository.

| Module | Version | License | Why |

| charm.land/bubbles/v2 (github.com/charmbracelet/bubbles) | v2.2.1 | MIT | Direct: command bar textarea, key bindings |

Also declared in `mac/Package.swift`. Not part of `IRIE_MAC_NO_SWIFTTERM=1` builds |

Not part of `IRIE_MAC_NO_GHOSTTY=1` builds |

SHA-256s: `xterm.js` 1f991ac3…2f7495, `xterm.css` ba8e6985…faf2b6, `addon-fit.js` bdaefa37…c97089, `addon-webgl.js` 9ffa9ac3…727cdb, `addon-unicode11.js` b0c3be54…a148d0. `vendor/NOTICES.txt` gathers their licenses with go-webview2's and golang.org/x/sys's; the app shows it under Settings > About, and the zip carries it |

The agent workflows (`docs/proposals/self-maintaining.md`) copy no code either. On GitHub's runners they run GitHub's own actions (`actions/checkout`, `actions/setup-go`, `actions/upload-artifact`, `actions/download-artifact`, `actions/create-github-app-token`; MIT), Claude Code and the Codex CLI from npm at the versions pinned in the workflows, and the runner's tmux, Go, Python and gh. Their glue, in `.github/irie/`, uses only the Python standard library.

`go.sum` also lists modules that only those dependencies' own tests use (github.com/MakeNowJust/heredoc, github.com/aymanbagabas/go-udiff, github.com/charmbracelet/x/exp/golden, golang.org/x/exp and others). They are not compiled into `irie`.

The website in `site/` has no third-party code: no scripts, fonts or stylesheets from elsewhere, and its pictures are drawn for it. The Pages workflow, `.github/workflows/pages.yml`, runs GitHub's own actions on GitHub's runners. Nothing from them is copied into the repository.

| Action | Version | License | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| actions/checkout | v7 | MIT | Pages workflow: fetch the repository |
| actions/configure-pages | v6 | MIT | Pages workflow: read the Pages settings |
| actions/upload-pages-artifact | v5 | MIT | Pages workflow: package `site/` |
| actions/deploy-pages | v5 | MIT | Pages workflow: publish it |

`.github/conventional-commit.sh`, `.github/workflows/commits.yml` and `.github/workflows/release-please.yml` are copied from the templates of the [Steak framework](https://github.com/irie-people/steak) (`templates/project`, MIT). The two workflows run these actions on GitHub's runners; nothing from them is copied into the repository.

| Action | Version | License | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| actions/checkout | v4 | MIT | commits workflow: fetch the pull request's history |
| googleapis/release-please-action | v4 | Apache-2.0 | release-please workflow: the release pull request, the tag and the GitHub release |

## Third-party text

Thirty-nine files hold text written elsewhere. The Code of Conduct keeps its own license and says so at its end. The charters' texts are in the public domain: the National Archives' transcriptions, the United States Code's text, a 1914 translation of Magna Carta printed in 1915, and the Universal Declaration of Human Rights, a United Nations document issued under a document symbol, which the UN leaves in the public domain (ST/AI/189/Add.9/Rev.2). The Petition of Right's, the English Bill of Rights' and the Freedom of Information Act 2000's texts as legislation.gov.uk sets them out are under the Open Government Licence v3.0, whose attribution each file's own about line carries; the Acts' words themselves are centuries out of copyright, as are the Habeas Corpus Act's in the 1819 edition British History Online keyed. Sweden's Freedom of the Press Act of 1766 is Ian Giles and Peter Graves's translation, under the Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International licence, credited in the file's about line with the licence's link, the layout being the only change. India's Right to Information Act is reproduced under section 52(1)(q)(ii) of India's Copyright Act, 1957, which makes the reproduction of any Act of a Legislature no infringement on the condition that it is reproduced together with commentary on it or other original matter: the file's own notes and about line, and Irie's words around the text, are that matter.

| File | Source | Version | License |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` | Contributor Covenant, stewarded by the Organization for Ethical Source, https://www.contributor-covenant.org/version/3/0/ | 3.0, adapted | CC BY-SA 4.0 |
| `internal/skills/bundled/founding-documents/declaration.md` | National Archives, https://www.archives.gov/founding-docs/declaration-transcript | As read on 2026-11-21 | Public domain |

## Third-party images

The Mac app carries Spotify's full logo, which Spotify's design guidelines ask an app to show beside Spotify's content. The files are Spotify's own, unaltered, and Spotify's marks stay Spotify's: Irie shows them only beside Spotify's content, as its Developer Terms allow.

| File | Source | Version | Terms |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mac/Resources/Spotify_Full_Logo_RGB_Black.png`, `mac/Resources/Spotify_Full_Logo_RGB_White.png` | https://developer.spotify.com/images/guidelines/design/2024-spotify-full-logo.zip, linked from https://developer.spotify.com/documentation/design | The 2024 logo files, byte for byte | Spotify's trademark, used under the Spotify Developer Terms (III.1.c) and its design guidelines |
